Q1. Digit sum \(14\) with tens digit \(7\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(76\)
B. \(79\)
C. \(77\)
D. \(78\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=14\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 77.

\[
N=10\times 7+7=77
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 77 is 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(77\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=77
\]

Fast result: \(77\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. Between \(112\) and \(238\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 112 is 112.

\[
8\times 14=112
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 232 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 238.

\[
238=8\times 29+6
\]

\[
238-6=232
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{232-112}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{238}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{111}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-13=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count
---
Q3. For the number \(1440\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)
C. \(36\)
D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(1440)=(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)=36
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_prime_factorization
---

Shortcut

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q5. Two cyclic events repeat every \(56\) and \(136\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1000\)
B. \(1047\)
C. \(904\)
D. \(952\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

\[
136=2^{3}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,136)=952
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 952.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(952\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,136)=\frac{56\times 136}{\operatorname{HCF}(56,136)}=952
\]

Fast result: \(952\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_hidden_hcf
---
Q6. For two numbers \(24\) and \(78\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(312\)
B. \(328\)
C. \(343\)
D. \(296\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,78)=312
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 312.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(312\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(24,78)=\frac{24\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(24,78)}=312
\]

Fast result: \(312\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q7. For exponent \(78\), what ending digit does \(23^{78}\) have?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
23\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(78-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
23^{78}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(78-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{78}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_unit_digit_cycle
---
Q8. For exponent \(116\), what ending digit does \(17^{116}\) have?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 7\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
7\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(116-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
17^{116}\equiv 1\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(116-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
7^{116}\equiv 1\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q9. With tens \(3\) and ones \(1\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+1=31
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+3=13
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-1|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-1|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_digit_interchange
---
Q10. A number leaves remainder \(5\) when divided by both \(6\) and \(12\). If it is the \(12\)-th such number after zero, what is the number?
A. \(156\)
B. \(164\)
C. \(142\)
D. \(149\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Translate every clue into a number condition

Reconstruction questions should not be guessed; each clue must become a checkable relation.

\[
N=12\times 12+5
\]

Step 2: Carry the result through the next condition

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
N=149
\]

Answer Verification
The reconstructed value is 149.
\[
N=149
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(149\).

Shortcut

Use the strongest clue first, then verify the candidate against the remaining condition.

\[
Z=149
\]

Fast result: \(149\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q11. In \(25!\), find the exponent of \(2\) in its prime factorisation?
A. \(21\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
12+6+3+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 25! is 22.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q13. The expression \(11^{114}\) is reduced modulo \(9\). What remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(114-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{114}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(114-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
2^{114}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q14. What least number should be subtracted from \(258\) so that the result is divisible by \(11\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
258=11\times 23+5
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 5.
\[
N=5
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q15. By what least number should \(108\) be multiplied to make it a perfect square?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{3}\to 3^{4}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=3
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_perfect_square_completion
---

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

\[

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q24. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors

Fast result: \(180\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q27. When \(3^{59}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(59-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
3^{59}\equiv 5\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(59-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
3^{59}\equiv 5\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_nested_remainder

Q29. Tens digit \(3\) and digit sum \(11\) are fixed. What is the number?

D. \(40\)

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 38.

\[
N=10\times 3+8=38
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 38 is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(38\).

Shortcut

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=38
\]

Fast result: \(38\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q31. When \(3^{52}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(52-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{52}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(52-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
3^{52}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(108)=(2+1)\times (3+1)=12
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (3+1)
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q41. Between \(181\) and \(278\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 181 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 276 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 278.

\[
278=12\times 23+2
\]

\[
278-2=276
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{276-192}{12}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{278}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{180}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-15=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q42. Bells ring at intervals of \(78\) and \(78\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(77\)
B. \(80\)
C. \(78\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=78
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 78.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=\frac{78\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,78)}=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q47. With tens \(7\) and ones \(6\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+6=76
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+7=67
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-6|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-6|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q59. Between \(137\) and \(259\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 137 is 144.

\[
9\times 16=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 259.

\[
259=9\times 28+7
\]

\[
259-7=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{252-144}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{259}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{136}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-15=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(27\)

C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+4=74
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+7=47
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-4|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-4|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_digit_interchange
---

Q87. Power \(13^{76}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(76-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
13^{76}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(76-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
4^{76}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{1}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{1}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=6
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q95. The number \(292x4\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q96. Two cyclic events repeat every \(198\) and \(108\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1307\)
B. \(1426\)
C. \(1069\)
D. \(1188\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
198=2\times 3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=1188
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1188.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1188\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=\frac{198\times 108}{\operatorname{HCF}(198,108)}=1188
\]

Fast result: \(1188\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q97. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q110. Between \(238\) and \(337\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 238 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 337.

\[
337=15\times 22+7
\]

\[
337-7=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{330-240}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{337}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{237}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-15=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(49\)

Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 27\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 43\rightarrow 29\rightarrow 87\rightarrow 61\rightarrow 83\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 47\rightarrow 41\rightarrow 23\rightarrow 69\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 21\rightarrow 63\rightarrow 89\rightarrow 67\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(90-1)\bmod 20+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 49.

\[
3^{90}\equiv 49\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 49.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(49\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 20+1=10
\]

\[
3^{90}\equiv 49\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(49\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q117. Between \(129\) and \(307\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 129 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 307.

\[
307=15\times 20+7
\]

\[
307-7=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-135}{15}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{307}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{128}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-8=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q137. The number \(414x6\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(75-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
4^{75}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q153. Between \(106\) and \(278\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(28\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(30\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 106 is 108.

\[
6\times 18=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 276 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 278.

\[
278=6\times 46+2
\]

\[
278-2=276
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{276-108}{6}+1=28+1=29
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 29.
Hence, there are 29 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(29\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{278}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{105}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
46-17=29
\]

Fast result: \(29\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: D

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Fast result: \(660\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---

Q171. Power \(13^{26}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(26-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
13^{26}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(26-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

\[
2^{26}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

\[
N=216
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(216\).

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q181. Between \(173\) and \(258\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 173 is 180.

\[
15\times 12=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 255 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 258.

\[
258=15\times 17+3
\]

\[
258-3=255
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{255-180}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{258}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{172}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-11=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Cycle length = 2.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(29-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{29}\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(29-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
3^{29}\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 218 is 222.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 378 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 378.

\[
378=6\times 63+0
\]

\[
378-0=378
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{378-222}{6}+1=26+1=27
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 27.
Hence, there are 27 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q218. The number \(692x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 21; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[

\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q223. When \(11^{38}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?

D. \(5\)
Answer: C

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 12+1=2
\]

\[

Q224. Digits \(6\) and \(1\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(46\)
B. \(44\)
C. \(47\)
D. \(45\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+1=61
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+6=16
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-1|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-1|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

B. \(12\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 111 is 120.

\[
12\times 10=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 255.

\[
255=12\times 21+3
\]

\[
255-3=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{252-120}{12}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{255}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{110}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-9=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

Answer Verification

\[

Q251. A number leaves the same remainder as \(7^{99}\) when divided by \(19\). What is that remainder?

C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(99-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{99}\equiv 1\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(99-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{99}\equiv 1\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q259. Between \(160\) and \(253\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 160 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 253.

\[
253=15\times 16+13
\]

\[
253-13=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{240-165}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{253}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{159}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-10=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Q278. Digit sum \(11\) with tens digit \(6\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(67\)

D. \(64\)

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 65.

\[
N=10\times 6+5=65
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 65 is 11.

Final Answer

u=5
\]

\[
N=65
\]

Fast result: \(65\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q279. Between \(232\) and \(331\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 232 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 331.

\[
331=9\times 36+7
\]

\[
331-7=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-234}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{331}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{231}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-25=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q280. Reduce the power \(7^{115}\) by its remainder pattern. What is the remainder on division by \(19\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(115-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{115}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(115-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{115}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q293. The number \(5x345\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q294. For the number \(480\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(1814\)
B. \(1361\)
C. \(1512\)
D. \(1663\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
7^{1}\to 7^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
252\times 7=1764
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=1764
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1764\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=7
\]

Fast result: \(1764\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q307. Between \(155\) and \(309\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 155 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 309.

\[
309=15\times 20+9
\]

\[
309-9=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-165}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{309}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{154}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-10=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
0
\]

\[

\]

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[

\]

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 35.

\[
N=10\times 3+5=35
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 35 is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(35\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=5
\]

\[
N=35
\]

Fast result: \(35\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q334. Between \(162\) and \(329\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 162 is 162.

\[
18\times 9=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 329.

\[
329=18\times 18+5
\]

\[
329-5=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-162}{18}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{329}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{161}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-8=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[

\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+3=63
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-6|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-6|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q342. The number \(610x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 13.

\[
13+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
8\rightarrow 64\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 96\rightarrow 68\rightarrow 44\rightarrow 52\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 28\rightarrow 24\rightarrow 92\rightarrow 36\rightarrow 88\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 32\rightarrow 56\rightarrow 48\rightarrow 84\rightarrow 72\rightarrow 76
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(92-1)\bmod 20+1=12
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 36.

\[
8^{92}\equiv 36\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 12th term of the cycle is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(92-1)\bmod 20+1=12
\]

\[
8^{92}\equiv 36\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 240 is 243.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 349.

\[
349=9\times 38+7
\]

\[
349-7=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{342-243}{9}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q368. The number \(358x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q386. In \(40!\), find the exponent of \(3\) in its prime factorisation?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 3 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{3}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{40}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
13+4+1=18
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

Q387. Between \(237\) and \(375\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 237 is 240.

\[
8\times 30=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 368 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 375.

\[
375=8\times 46+7
\]

\[
375-7=368
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{368-240}{8}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{375}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{236}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
46-29=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q388. The expression \(11^{76}\) is reduced modulo \(9\). What remainder is obtained?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(76-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
11^{76}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(76-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
2^{76}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q404. The number \(7x349\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=15
\]

\[
15-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
15-x=11
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
15-4=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q414. The number \(84x29\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?

Answer: D

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[

\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11+0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q416. Power \(11^{59}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?

D. \(7\)
Answer: A

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(59-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
11^{59}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(59-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
2^{59}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q425. Between \(103\) and \(239\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 103 is 108.

\[
6\times 18=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 234 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 239.

\[
239=6\times 39+5
\]

\[
239-5=234
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{234-108}{6}+1=21+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 22.
Hence, there are 22 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{239}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{102}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-17=22
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
12+2=14
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 60! = 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[
p=(61-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q445. Between \(127\) and \(276\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 127 is 128.

\[
8\times 16=128
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 272 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 276.

\[
276=8\times 34+4
\]

\[
276-4=272
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{272-128}{8}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{276}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{126}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-15=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q446. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(105,90)=630
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 630.

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(630\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Q457. The number \(732x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+7=87
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 333 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 337.

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(20\).

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q479. Two cyclic events repeat every \(84\) and \(204\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?

Answer: D

\[

\]

\[
204=2^{2}\times 3\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,204)=1428
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1428.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1428\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,204)=\frac{84\times 204}{\operatorname{HCF}(84,204)}=1428
\]

Fast result: \(1428\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

\[

\]

\[
x=7
\]

\[

\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Final Answer

\[
p=(113-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q498. Between \(227\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 227 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=18\times 19+5
\]

\[
347-5=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{342-234}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{226}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-12=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(160\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Answer: B

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-6|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-6|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q520. The number \(214x8\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q521. Two cyclic events repeat every \(60\) and \(156\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(819\)
B. \(858\)
C. \(741\)
D. \(780\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,156)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,156)=\frac{60\times 156}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,156)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q522. Power \(3^{45}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(45-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{45}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(45-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{45}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 25! = 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q530. The number \(12x05\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
4+x=11
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4+7=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(990\)
Answer: B

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
300=2^{2}\times 3\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{1}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
300\times 3=900
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(900\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(900\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=13
\]

\[
S+x=13+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

D. \(3\)
Answer: A

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(58-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{58}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(58-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{58}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q560. The number \(3x094\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q566. Between \(153\) and \(328\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(28\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(30\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 153 is 156.

\[
6\times 26=156
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 328.

\[
328=6\times 54+4
\]

\[
328-4=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{324-156}{6}+1=28+1=29
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 29.
Hence, there are 29 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(29\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{328}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{152}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
54-25=29
\]

Fast result: \(29\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[

\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
17-6=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Hence, the number is 33.

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

Fast result: \(33\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q576. Between \(114\) and \(265\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(25\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(27\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 114 is 114.

\[
6\times 19=114
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 265.

\[
265=6\times 44+1
\]

\[
265-1=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{264-114}{6}+1=25+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 26.
Hence, there are 26 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{265}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
44-18=26
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

\]

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
p=(101-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q583. Number \(89\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+9=89
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+8=98
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-9|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-9|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification

\[
19-8=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q594. The number \(6x650\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 17; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 5.
\[
N=5
\]

Final Answer

Q604. The number \(71x38\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q605. When \(2^{40}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(40-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
2^{40}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(40-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
2^{40}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\]

Final Answer

Q614. The number \(776x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 22.

\[
22+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q622. Between \(179\) and \(317\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 179 is 180.

\[
12\times 15=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 317.

\[
317=12\times 26+5
\]

\[
317-5=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{312-180}{12}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{317}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{178}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-14=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q633. Between \(150\) and \(275\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 150 is 150.

\[
6\times 25=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 275.

\[
275=6\times 45+5
\]

\[
275-5=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{270-150}{6}+1=20+1=21
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 21.
Hence, there are 21 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(21\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{275}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{149}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
45-24=21
\]

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(55-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{55}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(55-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{55}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q648. The number \(2x094\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q657. Between \(101\) and \(226\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 101 is 108.

\[
12\times 9=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 226.

\[
226=12\times 18+10
\]

\[
226-10=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{216-108}{12}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{226}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{100}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-8=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q660. When \(11^{56}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(56-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
11^{56}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(56-1)\bmod 12+1=8
\]

\[
11^{56}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).
