A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=19
\]

\[
S+x=19+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q675. The number \(89x14\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 11.

\[
11+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
11+x=15
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
11+4=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 224.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(224\).

Shortcut

\]

Fast result: \(224\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q684. Power \(11^{40}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(40-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{40}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(40-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
4^{40}\equiv 4\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 137 is 144.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 281.

\[
281=18\times 15+11
\]

\[
281-11=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{270-144}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{281}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{136}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-7=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

The 2nd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q715. Between \(225\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 225 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=18\times 19+5
\]

\[
347-5=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{342-234}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{224}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-12=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=990
\]

Final Answer

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(990\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q719. Number \(44\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+4=44
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+4=44
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-4|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-4|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q723. The number \(376x4\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
6-x=0
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6-6=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q733. The number \(8x098\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=25
\]

\[
S+x=25+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q736. The power \(23^{64}\) is very large. What are its final three digits?
A. \(485\)
B. \(419\)
C. \(441\)
D. \(463\)
Answer: C

Explanation:

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 24.

\[
23\rightarrow 529\rightarrow 167\rightarrow 841\rightarrow 343\rightarrow 889\rightarrow 447\rightarrow 281\rightarrow 463\rightarrow 649\rightarrow 927\rightarrow 321\rightarrow 383\rightarrow 809\rightarrow 607\rightarrow 961\rightarrow 103\rightarrow 369\rightarrow 487\rightarrow 201\rightarrow 623\rightarrow 329\rightarrow 567\rightarrow 41
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(64-1)\bmod 24+1=16
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 961.

\[
23^{64}\equiv 961\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 16th term of the cycle is 961.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(441\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(64-1)\bmod 24+1=16
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(441\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q737. If the digit sum is \(10\) and the tens digit is \(6\), what number is formed?
A. \(65\)
B. \(63\)
C. \(66\)
D. \(64\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=10\Rightarrow u=4
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 64.

\[
N=10\times 6+4=64
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 64 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(64\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=4
\]

\[
N=64
\]

Fast result: \(64\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(27\)

C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
252=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
7^{1}\to 7^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=27
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=7
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q743. The number \(21x38\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 14; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q746. With tens \(7\) and ones \(8\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+8=78
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 8+7=87
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-8|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-8|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q753. The number \(8x127\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=14
\]

\[
14-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
14-x=11
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
14-3=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[

Answer Verification

Final Answer

Q763. The number \(554x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

The required difference is 0.

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q773. Between \(193\) and \(298\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 193 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 298.

\[
298=15\times 19+13
\]

\[
298-13=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-195}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{298}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{192}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-12=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q777. The number \(970x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
15+3=18
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 5 dividing 75! is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{75}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{75}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q794. The number \(298x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 25.

\[
25+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
25+x=30
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
25+5=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q797. Tens digit \(3\) and ones digit \(3\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+3=33
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+3=33
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-3|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-3|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q801. The number \(129x7\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=15
\]

\[
15-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
15-x=11
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
15-4=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q807. Between \(136\) and \(230\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 136 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 230.

\[
230=15\times 15+5
\]

\[
230-5=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{225-150}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{230}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{135}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-9=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

C. \(5\)

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 22.

\[
22+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q838. Between \(157\) and \(315\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(26\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 157 is 162.

\[
6\times 27=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 315.

\[
315=6\times 52+3
\]

\[
315-3=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{312-162}{6}+1=25+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 26.
Hence, there are 26 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{315}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{156}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
52-26=26
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q842. The number \(47x92\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q843. Power \(3^{60}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(60-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{60}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(60-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{60}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q848. Between \(112\) and \(288\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 112 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 285 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 288.

\[
288=15\times 19+3
\]

\[
288-3=285
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{285-120}{15}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{288}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{111}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-7=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=17
\]

\[
17-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
17-x=11
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
17-6=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q866. The number \(992x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 24; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
24+x=27
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
24+3=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q867. Power \(13^{38}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
13^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_modular_cycle
---

Q873. The number \(13x58\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q874. Bells ring at intervals of \(30\) and \(36\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(198\)
B. \(171\)
C. \(180\)
D. \(189\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(30,36)=180
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 180.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(180\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(30,36)=\frac{30\times 36}{\operatorname{HCF}(30,36)}=180
\]

Fast result: \(180\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q883. The number \(432x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 73 is 10.

Final Answer

\]

\[

Fast result: \(73\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q897. The number \(2x870\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 17; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q898. For exponent \(94\), what ending digit does \(7^{94}\) have?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
7\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(94-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
7^{94}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(94-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
7^{94}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q914. The number \(976x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 26.

\[
26+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
26+x=30
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
26+4=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q917. Between \(139\) and \(270\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 139 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 270.

\[
270=15\times 18+0
\]

\[
270-0=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-150}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{270}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{138}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-9=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q922. Between \(123\) and \(228\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 123 is 128.

\[
8\times 16=128
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 224 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 228.

\[
228=8\times 28+4
\]

\[
228-4=224
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{224-128}{8}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{228}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{122}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-15=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q923. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q924. When \(13^{54}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(54-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{54}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(54-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{54}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q941. The number \(6x216\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(1474\)
D. \(1638\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=1638
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1638.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1638\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=\frac{126\times 234}{\operatorname{HCF}(126,234)}=1638
\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q948. The number \(132x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(9\)

Answer: B

\[

\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+6=76
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-3
\]

\[
-3+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-3+x=0
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-3+3=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q975. The number \(95x70\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=21
\]

\[
S+x=21+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q976. When \(13^{28}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
3^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q982. The number \(414x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q985. Between \(122\) and \(302\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 122 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 302.

\[
302=15\times 20+2
\]

\[
302-2=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-135}{15}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{302}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{121}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-8=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q999. The number \(532x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 10; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
8^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
8^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).
